Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. I hope it's okay to admit that. I just... I don't want you to think I'm not trying to be helpful or that I'm not paying attention. It's just...

Um... yes, I suppose so. I really hate to admit it, to be honest with you. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, because I really do try to push through it and be strong.

I feel terrible even saying it out loud, I know how pathetic it must sound to you. I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for feeling this way. I'm trying to be better, I promise. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm giving you the right information.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yes, I think so. I'm sorry, I feel like I'm repeating myself a lot, and I really don't want to waste your time. I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to hear. 

But yes, to answer your question... it was definitely after. Like I was trying to say earlier, before the house problems really dragged on and I fell so deeply into this... this depression, I never heard those voices in the walls. And I never saw the wet patches, or... or had those awful thoughts about the neighbors tracking me. I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying that. 

Those things... the voices and the patches... they only showed up after things got really bad. After I had already completely stopped caring about my usual life, and was just waking up feeling like a total failure every single day. 

I hope that answers your question properly. Is that the kind of detail you were looking for? I can try to explain it differently if that wasn't clear enough.

It started right after that bad winter freeze we had. It kind of... well, it turned all these house repairs I was trying to do into this huge mess, and I just couldn't get out from under it. I know I probably sound like I'm just making excuses for falling behind, and I really don't mean to. Is that... does that timeline make sense for this sort of thing?\n\nDoctor: Do the whispering voices you hear ever say anything that's related to your mood or how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose they do. I mean, the timing definitely lines up with how bad I've been feeling. I hope I'm explaining this right, but... before those house problems dragged on and I really fell into this depression, I never heard any voices in the walls. Or saw those wet patches that weren't there. \n\nAnd, um... I'm really sorry, I probably should have mentioned this earlier, I was just so embarrassed and didn't want you to think I was completely losing my mind... but I also started thinking the neighbors were tracking me. I know how ridiculous that sounds, I really do. Please don't think terribly of me. \n\nBut none of those strange things ever happened before. They only showed up after I had already stopped caring about my usual life, you know? It was only after I just started feeling like such a failure every single day that the voices and everything else started. I hope that answers your question... does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear or the things you see ever tell you or show you something that's related to your feelings of being a failure?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm understanding you right, but the voices... they don't really say anything about me being a failure. They're just sort of... whispering about the pipes, or the water pressure, things like that. It's mostly just mumbling that I can't quite make out, to be honest. \n\nAnd the wet patch on the floor, it's just... a wet patch. It doesn't show me anything about myself. I'm sorry if that's not what you were looking for. I know I'm probably not explaining this very well. I just... I haven't noticed them saying anything mean about me, if that's what you mean. Is that a good thing? I hope that's a good sign.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or find the right words when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. I hope it's okay to admit that. I just... I don't want you to think I'm not trying to be helpful or that I'm not paying attention. It's just... \n\nMy wife, she actually mentioned it the other day. She said I move like I'm underwater now. And... well, even just answering a simple question, like about a repair estimate or something, it can take me so long to get the words out that people just start talking for me. I feel terrible about it, really. I know it must be so frustrating for everyone around me, and for you too, probably, having to wait on me to finish a thought. \n\nI really am trying my best to keep up, I promise. I just... my brain feels so slow lately. Does that make sense? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling persistently sad or empty for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past seven months?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I suppose so. I really hate to admit it, to be honest with you. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, because I really do try to push through it and be strong. \n\nBut... well, yes. Most days, I wake up and I just... I already feel like I've completely failed. Like I've failed at being a husband, and just... at being a man in general. And that heaviness, it just sits right on me from the moment I open my eyes until I finally go to bed. \n\nI feel terrible even saying it out loud, I know how pathetic it must sound to you. I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for feeling this way. I'm trying to be better, I promise. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices or the things you see ever seem to match or reflect how you're feeling at the time?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I really hope I'm understanding you right, but the... the voices and the wet patch, they don't really seem to change based on how I'm feeling. \n\nI mean, even on the days when I'm feeling a little bit better—and I do try to have good days, I really do—the mumbling about the pipes is still just... there. And the wet patch on the floor, it's always the same, no matter if I'm feeling really down or if I'm trying to be positive. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably not a very helpful answer. I just haven't noticed any kind of connection like that. I hope it's okay that they don't match up. Does that make sense? I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of details.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices or the things you see ever seem to be related to your feelings of sadness or emptiness?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's kind of like I was saying before, I guess. I hope you don't mind me repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. \n\nBut no, they don't really seem connected to the sadness. The voices, the mumbling... they don't really talk about me being a failure or feeling empty, if that's what you mean. They just keep going on about the pipes and the water. And the wet patch is just... it's just there on the floor. \n\nI'm sorry, I know you asked something similar just a moment ago. I hope I'm not frustrating you by not having a better answer. I really am trying to pay close attention to them for you, but they just seem to do their own thing, no matter how I'm feeling inside. Is that alright? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and the wet patch start at the same time as your feelings of sadness and emptiness?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, they didn't start at exactly the same time. I hope I'm explaining this right for you. Before all the... the issues with the house dragged on and I really fell into this depression, I never heard any voices in the walls. And I never saw any wet patches that weren't there, or... well, or thought the neighbors were tracking me. \n\nI know how awful that last part sounds, I really do, and I'm so sorry to even bring it up. I don't want you to think I'm completely losing my mind or being difficult. But... yeah, those things, they only really showed up after I had already stopped caring about my usual life. They started after I was already waking up feeling like a failure every day. \n\nI hope that's helpful. Am I giving you the right kind of timeline? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices and visions would stop if your feelings of sadness and emptiness went away?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I really hope so. I mean, it makes sense, right? I hope I'm not overstepping by guessing, but... well, like I mentioned just a minute ago, I never had any of these issues before I got so incredibly down about the house. \n\nI never used to hear those voices in the walls or see the wet spots on the floor before all of this. And... and I certainly never had those embarrassing thoughts about the neighbors watching me. I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for that part, I know it sounds so strange. \n\nBut since none of that started until after I felt so completely defeated and stopped caring about my life... I'd like to think that if I could just get back to being myself, and stop waking up feeling like such a failure, maybe those other things would fade away too. Does that sound right to you? I really want to believe that's how it works. I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and visions appear after your feelings of sadness and emptiness became severe?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I think so. I'm sorry, I feel like I'm repeating myself a lot, and I really don't want to waste your time. I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to hear. \n\nBut yes, to answer your question... it was definitely after. Like I was trying to say earlier, before the house problems really dragged on and I fell so deeply into this... this depression, I never heard those voices in the walls. And I never saw the wet patches, or... or had those awful thoughts about the neighbors tracking me. I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying that. \n\nThose things... the voices and the patches... they only showed up after things got really bad. After I had already completely stopped caring about my usual life, and was just waking up feeling like a total failure every single day. \n\nI hope that answers your question properly. Is that the kind of detail you were looking for? I can try to explain it differently if that wasn't clear enough.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and visions start after you had already been feeling sad and empty for a while?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. Yes, they did. I... I know we keep circling back to this, and I really hope I'm not frustrating you by saying the same things over and over. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. \n\nBut yes, it was definitely a while after. Like I mentioned, before the house problems dragged on for so long and I really fell into this... this deep rut, I never experienced any of this. That's when the other things started happening. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that clearly enough for you. Is that what you were asking? I can try to be more specific if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Did the feelings of sadness and emptiness start before the voices and visions appeared?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... yes. Yes, they did. I'm so sorry, I must be doing a really terrible job of explaining this if I'm making you ask it again. I promise I'm not trying to be difficult or confusing! Am I answering your questions okay? Um... most of every day? I... I wouldn't want to exaggerate and make it sound worse than it is. I mean, they are there a lot, like I said, but... I don't want you to think I'm completely out of touch with reality from morning until night.
